relations between the european union and the republic of seychelles in the field of fisheries are governed by a sustainable fisheries partnership agreement which entered into force on 10 may 2003 and has been renewed twice since then.
— relations between the european union and the republic of seychelles fisheries are governed by a partnership agreement in the field of fisheries sustainability, which respects the rules of the common fisheries policy of the european union and thereby permits greater transparency than the previous private agreement.
the eu and tunisia signed a euro-mediterranean association agreement in 1995, which has been in force since 1998, and which includes a regular dialogue on political and security issues, economic, trade and financial cooperation - focusing mainly on the progressive liberalisation of trade - sustainable development of the region and investment, and social, cultural and educational cooperation, in particular through intercultural dialogue, control of migration, skills development, promotion of labour law and gender equality.

the spanish socialist delegation supports the agreement to give flexibility to member states wishing to ban gmos on their territory because it considers that the new directive complies with the eu's basic principles regarding gmos: the precautionary principle, transparency and the protection of health and the environment.
in this period, which will begin next spring, it will be necessary to take note of the effectiveness of the coexistence measures which will now be binding in order to see whether the directive actually fulfils its task of preventing unwanted pollution, especially in the case of traditional and organic farming.

the aim of the report is to transpose into european law the recommendations adopted by the general fisheries commission for the mediterranean in 2011-2013. these recommendations relate to the sustainable exploitation of red coral, the mitigation of bycatch of seabirds, sea turtles, monk seals and cetaceans and the protection of sharks and rays, fragile species and ecosystems that we need to protect and carefully manage.

the resolution tabled by the s&d group calls on the commission to tackle the following priorities: an investment plan to generate more jobs and growth, a fiscal environment that favours member states' investment in the european fund for strategic investments or the inclusion of a social dimension.
the commission must also work towards an energy union with a dominant role for renewable energy, tackle a european immigration policy and avoid tax fraud and tax havens.
the commission's work programme delves into the strategies of deregulation, liberalisation and economic governance that have led to the current social, economic and environmental crisis and the deep emptying of european democracy.

mr president, the fight against climate change also involves radically changing the way we eat.
that is why small-scale peasant farming, local food consumption and the empowerment of short marketing circuits, while they can support millions of jobs worldwide, will cause a drop in global temperature because they will contribute in a drastic way to a very considerable reduction in greenhouse gas emissions.
if we want to take action to tackle the serious climate crisis, we also have an obligation to commit ourselves in paris to a radical change in our agricultural and food policies and to cancel the ttip and ceta negotiations.
on the other hand, if we can choose to buy short circuits, we are making an important contribution to the fight against climate change, which apparently concerns us so much.
but we are also taking action against energy waste, which is no small thing considering that we have reached peak oil.

i voted in favour of the sustainable fisheries partnership agreement between the european union and the republic of senegal as it is part of the type of agreements which aim to enhance sustainable fisheries by promoting better exploitation of available stocks through the use of industry best practices.
this is a sustainable fisheries partnership agreement which replaces the one that expired in 2006. it lays down new conditions concerning, among others, the volume of catches and the economic contribution.
it will support the development of sustainable fisheries policies in the republic of senegal and will enable the senegalese authorities to improve the surveillance of their territorial waters, through a satellite monitoring system, to combat illegal, unreported and unregulated fishing.
this protocol will last for five years, will have a financial contribution of eur 13 930 000 and will be beneficial to both parties, as it will help both the republic of senegal and the european union to create new jobs and will advance sustainable fisheries and good governance.
the new agreement replaces the 1980 agreement for a renewable period of five years and allows the return of the european fleet to this fishery, from which it had been excluded since the suspension of the previous agreement in 2006. i voted in favour of this because it is a protocol of great importance for the spanish freezer tuna fleet, which will have 16 fishing licences, and will offer permits for seven spanish tuna-fishing vessels, based in bermeo (vizcaya), which already used the port of dakar as a base for their operations in the atlantic and operated in senegal under private agreements.

i truly believe that european unity, both in terms of facing the necessity to create a sustainable society, where we are less dependent on fossil fuels, where we are not in a position to be blackmailed by outside fuel providers, where we are able to create an economy that does not depend as much on fossil fuels and does use more renewable fuel sources, that is a strong contribution to a better life of our citizens, leads to greater stability within our member states, but especially to a stronger position in the evermore competitive world.

‒ i voted in favour of this resolution on the amended agreement establishing the general fisheries commission for the mediterranean, despite not being in favour of the provisions applicable to fisheries in the gfcm, as the agreement revises the structure and content of the current one by introducing a clearer explanation of the purpose of the gfcm agreement (defining a clear overall objective of ecological, social, economic and environmental sustainability of marine resources), better defines the functions of the gfcm and creates a well-defined dispute settlement mechanism.
‒ the gfcm is one of the so-called regional fisheries management organisations (rfmos) whose purpose is to promote the development, conservation, rational management and better exploitation of living marine resources, as well as the sustainable development of aquaculture, in the mediterranean and the black sea.

that is why we said that this directive is more than just an instrument, it is an emblematic example of how to achieve the transition to more sustainable production, in which - from design to waste - more environmentally friendly materials are chosen, alternatives are sought, pollution is paid for and reuse and responsible collection are encouraged.
madam president, commissioner, plastic bags - as has already been said here - are the source of many environmental problems, especially at sea.

preventing waste is the first link in the circular economy model that we believe in and that has to be our new industrial revolution.
given the wide disparities in the consumption of plastic bags between member states, measures such as the distinction of ultra-light bags, the promotion of new alternative materials, labelling to identify certain types of bags and the recommendation of a unit price at the point of sale will be, in addition to the binding targets, essential steps to achieve the minimisation of waste that the circular economy challenge imposes on us.

‒ the european forestry strategy highlights the importance of forests and forestry not only from an environmental point of view but also from an economic and social point of view.
it also proposes to improve the coordination of the various european policies involved (energy, research and development, employment, climate, etc.)
the european forest strategy sets targets for 2020, including the sustainable management of forests and the reduction of deforested or degraded land, and sets out a number of priority areas for action, such as coordination and communication on forest-related issues, support for rural and urban society from the forestry sector, improving the competitiveness of goods and services generated by forests, the relationship between forest area and climate change, and the development of innovative initiatives in the sector.
the resolution on a new eu strategy for forests and the forestry sector calls for a new strategy focused on coordination, so that forestry and forests fit better into the different european policies (energy, environment and climate protection, and r & d) and a more coherent approach can be defined, for which it calls for the strengthening of the standing forestry committee.

‒ the kyoto protocol to the united nations framework convention on climate change was signed in 1997 with the aim of setting an internationally binding emission reduction target.
the eu ratified this protocol in 2015. in 2012, the doha climate change conference approved an amendment to the kyoto protocol 1 that establishes a second period (2013 to 2020) with legally binding emission reduction commitments for the parties.
the amendment commits the eu member states and iceland to reducing their average annual greenhouse gas emissions.
i have supported this report which gives its consent to the doha amendment as a positive step, although i believe that the amended kyoto protocol is still quite far from effectively combating climate change on its own, as it lacks complementary measures, such as climate justice provisions, as well as serious work commitments by the most industrialized countries.

‒ the report is merely a procedure to allow the formal entry into force of the doha amendment when the eu, and subsequently the member states, have completed their planned ratification processes by the third quarter of this year at the latest, so that the instruments of acceptance are on the table for the decisive paris climate summit in december.
this amendment to the kyoto protocol (decided at the 2012 doha summit) establishes a second commitment period, which started in january 2013 and should run until december 2020. these commitments imply that the eu and the member states commit themselves to ensuring that the average of their emissions over this period is capped at 80% of the emissions in the base year (1990) and always taking as a global the total of the emissions allowed under the emissions trading system (ets).

‒ the integration of a market stability reserve offers a range of structural reforms to strengthen the existing emissions trading system (ets).
to name a few of the changes, a part of the allowances will start to be reinvested in a new innovation fund, through which the transition of industry towards the use of low-carbon technologies will be promoted.
a monitoring mechanism for the functioning of the reserve will also be included, which will assess its contribution to job creation, the prevention of carbon leakage and the industrial competitiveness of the union every three years.
the reform of the system makes it possible to continue moving towards a less ambiguous and more sustainable system, and that is why i am voting in favour of the proposal.
‒ i voted in favour of the creation of the emission allowance market stability reserve because, although the ets is a system that needs to be modified and corrected, i have no doubt that it remains an excellent instrument for combating climate change.
our criticism must always be constructive so that we can correct the inadequacy, but remain united against climate change and work towards meeting our goals, both those of the eu and those agreed on at global level.
on the other hand, at a time when european innovation, research and science are being punished by cuts, i welcome the fact that the agreement takes into account our idea of allocating funds from the reserve to industrial innovation actions and demonstration projects for low-carbon technologies such as co2 capture and storage.
‒ we voted against the ets and the 'trusted price' for carbon, because we believe that carbon is good for the oil and gas industry in the long term.
companies that rely on fossil fuels use the existence of the cdec as an argument for the elimination of eu energy efficiency and renewable energy targets.
however, the eu emissions trading system is designed to prolong dependence on fossil fuels and to expand and strengthen the role of financial markets in people's daily lives.
these fossil energy sources not only generate a considerable volume of unquantified emissions, but also have significant negative impacts on the environment, social aspects and health.
‒ i could not vote in favour of this report because, although it is a modification aimed at making the eu's emissions trading system more efficient, market mechanisms cannot be the only means available to reduce emissions from companies.
these market mechanisms are the only proposal for eu action in this area and have proved to be a total failure, allowing the big polluters to increase pollution and even their speculative profits.
action on emissions requires the strengthening of public, binding and coercive measures to make the polluter pay.

our decision is based on three ideas in the report: 1) guarantees for a just transition and that this principle should serve as a basis for negotiation at the paris climate conference cop 21; 2) the ultimate goal of promoting the creation of quality and sustainable jobs, integrating displaced and low-skilled workers into the strategy; 3) the transition must not have a negative impact on social security systems or on low-income people.

‒ i have decided to vote in favour of this report because it represents a major step forward in the consideration and treatment of waste as a resource for the european economy.
this proposal is fundamental to the reshaping of the european economy, it seeks to promote an activity based on greater economic sustainability, and it even calls for sanctions in cases of planned obsolescence.
the debate on waste generation and waste management is necessary to put an end to the environmental madness that has been the result of the capitalist industrial development of european countries.
‒ the commission presented in july 2014 its communication 'towards a circular economy: a zero waste agenda for europe', which contained ideas to contribute, through existing legislation and new initiatives, to creating a circular economy by optimising all processes in the life of a product and minimising waste.
this own-initiative report, which i voted in favour of, sets out parliament's first position on the priorities to be set for moving towards this circular economy, namely the need to set mandatory targets for the reduction of different types of waste by 2025, a specific target for organic waste by 2020, the gradual reduction of waste dumping, the adoption of the cascading principle for biomass in particular, the increase of recycling and reuse targets to at least 70% for urs and 80% for packaging by 2030, the strict limitation of incineration by 2020, an indicative target of 50% reduction of marine litter by 2025, a binding target of 30% resource efficiency by 2025 and the strengthening of ecodesign measures for all types of products.

‒ the unsustainable use of resources is causing environmental damage and also poses an economic risk, both for the eu and for the rest of the world, as the world economy uses the equivalent of a planet and a half in terms of resources to generate global output and absorb waste.
there is no doubt that if we continue along this path we will end up in an ecological and economic dead end, so we must use fewer resources and use them more efficiently.
resource efficiency is a solution to the ecological dilemma we face and to the economic challenges europe is grappling with.
as always, the principles and intentions behind the commission's proposals and the envi report are commendable: to re-examine our modes of consumption and production which have led us to this globalisation which is destroying our jobs and our environment.
however, the paths being considered do not even claim to tackle the real cause: the uprooting of our economy and industries, their relocation to less social and environmental concerns.

we are facing a paradigm shift and it is necessary to set binding waste reduction targets and to gradually reduce all types of waste disposal.

‒ i abstained from voting on this joint motion for a resolution on the situation in burundi because, although the text contains aspects with which i agree, such as the description of the climate of repression in the country, the concern about human rights violations and criticism of the electoral situation, the lack of transparent information and a call for dialogue and the cessation of violence, other key elements are missing, such as a more consistent criticism of neo-colonial practices and the exploitation of natural resources, as well as a deeper and more complex analysis of the origins of the current situation in the country.

mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to draw the attention of this house to one of the most dangerous latent threats to biodiversity in the union: the apple snail, which arrived in the left delta of the ebro in 2009 and prompted a commission decision in 2012 to combat it, has expanded its breeding area.

‒ the purpose of this regulation is to implement the 2009 recommendations and resolutions following the actions brought by canada and norway on seal products by removing the so-called orm exemption which allowed the marketing of seal products for the purpose of sustainable management on a small scale and without a profit motive.
this prohibition had two exceptions: the inuit exception, which allowed trade in products obtained from seals hunted by the inuit and other indigenous communities, and the marine resource management exception, which allowed trade in products obtained from hunting for the sole purpose of sustainable, small-scale and non-profit-making management of marine resources.
however, it provided for two exceptions: products of seals hunted by indigenous communities such as the inuit and derivatives of seals hunted not for commercial reasons but for the sustainable management of marine stocks and resources.

under the new protocol, the eu fleet has been able to resume its fishing activities in guinea-bissau after an interruption of over two years, and these fishing activities are sustainable: the stocks are considered to be in a good state and fisheries indicators such as catch levels and fishing effort have remained stable over the past years.
as mr van dalen mentioned, the sustainability of shark fishing was a concern under the previous protocol.
they also promote more sustainable and controlled fishing practices and are a key instrument in the fight against unregulated and unreported fishing.

if we do not take our place in these forums and in the oceans, other fleets far less concerned with sustainability than the eu fleets will do it for us.
we should be pleased that for the first time in the rules of the common fisheries policy there is a section devoted to sustainable fisheries partnership agreements.
on the one hand, they ensure the continuation of economic activity of the union fleet through sustainable exploitation and, on the other hand, they help the development of the third country's fishing sector in very different ways.
on the question of sharks, i would point out that the protocol already provides for measures to ensure the sustainability of the stocks in cape verde, and we will have a report next year, in accordance with the decision taken by the joint committee in march this year.
and i would also say that the reports of the iccat, which is the body responsible for monitoring shark stocks, state that both the bluefin tuna and the spotted kingfisher, which are the two species that are fished in cape verde, are not overexploited and that the biomass is well above that which guarantees maximum sustainable yield.
the european union’s fisheries partnership agreements (fpas) promote sustainability and the development of the partner countries’ fisheries sector, particularly in these developing countries, and they are not simply a transfer of money in exchange for access to fishery resources.
firstly, the sustainable fisheries partnership agreements are a regulated and transparent tool which allows us to frame the activity of the eu fleet in partner-country waters in full observance of the principles of sustainability, good governance and fairness.
i would like to conclude by confirming that these agreements are beneficial to the eu fishing sector and to the partner countries, and also in terms of promoting more sustainable fishing outside the eu and strengthening the fight against illegal, unreported and unregulated (iuu) fishing through the introduction of better controls, monitoring, supervision, scientific research and evaluation.

‒ the approximately 150,000 medium combustion plants in the eu are a major source of sulphur dioxide, nitrogen oxides and particulate matter emissions, with serious consequences for human health and the environment.
the objective of this directive is to control and reduce emissions of sulphur, nitrogen and particulate matter.
the agreement reached with the council sets a ceiling for emissions of sulphur dioxide, nitrogen oxides and particulate matter from existing combustion plants with a thermal output of more than 5 mw, which will come into force from 2025. the commission will also consider the possibility of extending the scope of the directive to include the use of renewable energy sources.

‒ although pollutant emissions have decreased considerably in recent decades, air pollution levels remain problematic in many parts of europe, and eu citizens continue to be exposed to air pollutants that can endanger their health and well-being.
in particular, pollutant emissions from medium combustion plants are not regulated at union level, despite their increasing contribution to air pollution.

‒ this resolution addresses the catastrophe that occurred on 4 october 2010 in hungary, when the rupture of a waste raft released almost one million cubic metres of red mud (a highly alkaline extractive waste) that flooded several villages and left 10 people dead, 150 injured and large areas of land contaminated, including four natura 2000 sites.
i voted in favour of the resolution because, among other things, it points to the risk that a similar catastrophe could occur in several member states due to poor enforcement of environmental legislation; calls for more inspections of waste rafts; highlights the existence of serious environmental pollution problems in several member states due to mining activities (e.g.
from the use of cyanide in gold mining); calls on the commission, in the context of the current review of the environmental liability directive, to ensure that the proposed review fully applies the polluter-pays principle or involves local authorities, citizens and civil society in the decision-making process on the disposal of hazardous waste and in the planning of risk management measures.
‒ the break-up of a waste raft at a mining company on 4 october 2010 triggered a one million cubic metre red mud tide that caused ten deaths, 150 injuries and flooding of several municipalities and vast areas of land, including four high ecological value areas, and highlighted the lack of implementation of eu legislation and major deficiencies in the inspections framework.
as he rightly points out, disaster prevention needs to be strengthened through research, the development of hazardous waste treatment and the implementation of stricter environmental inspections.

‒ i voted in favour of the resolution because i considered the serious consequences of the breakdown of a waste raft in hungary which released almost one million cubic metres of highly alkaline red mud, flooding several villages, killing 10 people, injuring nearly 150 and polluting large areas of land.
despite the tragedy, similar sites still exist in several member states, and we must ensure that appropriate inspections are carried out and that red sludge is correctly classified as hazardous waste.
‒ five years after the environmental disaster in hungary, it is a good time to recall the importance of respecting european environmental legislation.
in this resolution we therefore call on the commission and the member states to step up their efforts to ensure the full implementation and proper application of all eu legislation and all international conventions relating to the environmentally sound management of hazardous waste.
‒ we voted in favour of this resolution, which seeks to take stock of where we are and where a stricter waste policy should be headed, now that five years have passed since the accident in hungary.
here we summarize the question in its expository phase: on 4 october 2010, the breaking of a waste raft in hungary released almost one million cubic metres of highly alkaline red mud.
we hope that this question and the subsequent resolution will serve as a wake-up call to the commission and that it will focus its efforts on evaluating the methods of evaluating waste bags so that these catastrophes are not repeated.

madam president, two ideas for this report on climate change.
80% of measures to promote energy saving, sustainable mobility, waste reduction and recycling, or citizen involvement in these efforts, are taken at local or regional level.

secondly, let us promote global agreements to avoid competitive differentials based on production that ignore global climate change objectives.
the european industry needs us to be extremely vigilant and to take action against the sale of products which, by their manufacture or characteristics, do not comply with the measures that our companies do comply with to combat climate change.

‒ my vote in favour of the pargneaux report is a strong endorsement of the negotiating mandate of the european parliament delegation to the united nations conference on climate change (cp21) in paris from 30 november to 11 december.
the socialists strongly support an active eu role at the paris summit with the aim of reaching an ambitious and legally binding agreement on emissions reductions, as well as sound financial instruments for achieving this objective.
as the date of the paris summit approaches, there is a need to step up climate diplomacy.
world leaders must understand that limiting global warming to a maximum of two degrees will not only help prevent climate change and the associated natural disasters, but will also prevent a dramatic increase in the number of refugees as a result of climate change.
on the other hand, the eu's 20/20/20 targets for emissions, renewable energy and energy savings demonstrate that there is no contradiction between growth and sustainability, as evidenced by the fact that the european economy has grown by 45% since 1990.
in writing.

however, i believe that giving priority to implementation through market instruments and, in particular, financial market instruments is incompatible with the commitment to reduce emissions effectively.
on the contrary, they will accelerate the breakdown of the traditional productive fabric, without in any case ensuring a transition to an environmentally and socially sustainable economy.

‒ i voted in favour of the text because it raises the need for the eu to devote more economic and technological resources to the fight against climate change and to establish and strengthen legally binding commitments in paris to reduce emissions of co2 and other gases that contribute to the greenhouse effect.
however, while the report addresses the problem that co2 emissions from transport have continued to rise, it does not introduce any criticism of free trade or the atci and leaves everything to the development of clean transport technologies.
as regards the defence of co2 emissions trading, this is a system which in my view creates a new economic bubble through the buying and selling of emission allowances and which is proving ineffective in meeting the objectives for which it was theoretically conceived, which is why we have spoken out against this system.

‒ i abstained on this report despite the fact that it calls for legal obligations to reduce greenhouse gas emissions rather than the non-binding commitments which unfortunately are the usual norm in this area.
the priority given to implementing them through market instruments and, in particular, the financial market is incompatible with the commitment to reduce emissions effectively.
in addition, they will accelerate the breakdown of the traditional productive fabric, without in any case ensuring a transition to an environmentally and socially sustainable economy.
although the current model of agricultural production and food marketing contributes significantly to greenhouse gas emissions, there is no proposal to move to an agro-ecological production model or to promote and enhance short marketing cycles.

‒ i strongly support such a resolution because climate change is now recognized worldwide as a top urgency.
the 2015 protocol must be legally binding and ambitious from the outset when adopted in paris, and should aim at phasing out global carbon emissions by 2050 or shortly thereafter so as to keep the world on a cost-effective emission trajectory compatible with the below 2°c target, and that a global ghg emissions peak will be reached as soon as possible.
the agreement must provide a predictable framework which encourages investments and scaling by business of efficient carbon reductions and adaptation technologies;only an ambitious and legally binding international agreement would help to address the carbon leakage and competitiveness concerns of the relevant sectors and in particular the energy intensive sector.
i call for a general reinvigoration of the eu’s climate policy, which would help build momentum in international climate discussions and would be in line with the upper limit of the eu’s commitment to reducing its ghg emissions to 80-95 % below 1990 levels by 2050.

‒ the socialists support the european commission's plans to impose stricter and more binding limits on emissions of air pollutants, which must be reached between 2020 and 2030. air pollution has enormous human, environmental and economic costs, with around 400 000 people dying prematurely in the eu each year from it.
‒ the revised proposal for an nec directive replaces the current union regime for the annual levelling of national emissions of air pollutants, as defined in directive 2001/81/ec, for four air pollutants, namely sulphur dioxide (so2), nitrogen oxides (nox), volatile organic compounds other than methane (vocs) and ammonia (nh3), until 2020. the revised nec directive sets out the controls on the different types of air pollution in each member state.
it aims to halve by 2030 the number of premature deaths - estimated at 400 000 a year in the eu - caused by air pollution.

in addition, member states shall draw up national air pollution control programmes.

the key points of the envi committee necd proposal are: - introduction of national ceilings for six pollutants (sulphur dioxide, nitrogen oxides, ammonia, methane, particulate matter and non-methane volatile organic compounds) by 2030 - mercury (hg) added to the list of pollutants as caps were introduced on mercury from 2020- midpoint targets for 2025 fully binding for all pollutants, with the exception of methane - methane targets delayed until 2030 to give the agriculture sector adaptation time - removal of ce proposal for flexibility allowing member states to offset reductions in emissions from nitrogen oxides, sulphur dioxides and particulate matter from international shipping - public participation, transparency, access to justice and information this ep report is stronger than the ec-proposal, at the same time that the big aim of the directive is to put controls on different types of air-pollution with the goal to cut the number of premature deaths (about 400 000/year, caused by air pollution) by half by 2030. hence, eh bildu has voted in favour.

the agreement reached at cop21 is an agreement that remains unresolved on climate change for several reasons: 1.commitments to reduce greenhouse gas emissions are not binding on states and, if they continue to be so, global warming will continue to increase.
2.it continues to focus on the commercialisation of the climate by maintaining instruments such as the carbon market, which allows those with money to pay to continue emitting greenhouse gases.
3.the consumption model, the development model, the industrialization of agriculture and the world trade model, which is driven by wasteful energy and resources, are not being questioned.
5.governments have failed, multinationals have won, the world has lost, but people will continue their struggles until they achieve another development based on responsible consumption, renewable energy, food sovereignty, short marketing circuits, relocation of production, sustainable mobility and divestment from fossil fuels.

‒ the spanish socialists have worked hard to ensure that the list of invasive species, which was voted on on 4 december in the joint committee on the environment, member states and the european commission, includes the species eichhornia crassipes, commonly known as the 'camel'.
the proliferation of this plant is seriously endangering the habitats and biodiversity of the guadiana river basin as it passes through extremadura, and is already moving towards portugal.
i have therefore voted against the rejection of the above-mentioned implementing regulation, because i consider it to be based on procedural arguments and not legally binding and, very specifically, because my main objective in this matter is the implementation, as quickly as possible, of measures to eliminate and combat the proliferation of this species and the speedy recovery of the guadiana river ecosystem.
we voted in favour considering that some invasive species have not been included in the list submitted by the commission although they should have been and that some of those included do not cause a significant negative impact on biodiversity/ecosystem.
however, this list includes species that pose a serious threat to our biodiversity, such as the water jasmine or camalote, which is currently a huge problem in the guadiana river and specifically in extremadura.
we must bear in mind that the draft union list does not solve the problem of invasive alien species in a comprehensive way, so that native biodiversity and ecosystem services are protected, and we must therefore continue to work towards their eradication.
